A modifier for a state (hungry, cold, without water, weak) is read from that state when shown; one for an event (newly married, a child born, grieving) is written at the event with its end; "talked with a friend" is read from the last talk with a friend, as it was judged at the talk: talk keeps its hour beside the latest talk (`friendAt`, at the friend line 0.25), since the latest talk alone hid two in three of them, so no talk writes for it but the one field (backlog VB-062) | Decided, 30 Oct (mood shown); how it is kept, Proposed, 15 Nov; the last friend's talk, Proposed (VB-062, 17 Nov); the allowance read, Proposed (VB-875, 22 Nov) |

#### Stories (Decided, 3 November: the model; the numbers Proposed, 15 November, from the experiment)

| Aspect | Design |
| --- | --- |
| What a story is | An event of the record of a kind worth telling, its subject (a pawn), the other party if any, and a valence. The first kinds are those opinion already remembers first-hand, each in a domain: law (a claim taken, -0.5; cattle let into a crop, -0.3; a work day shirked, -0.2), kin (a match refused, -0.3; a quarrel, -0.2; a child outside marriage, -0.3) and dealings (help given in hunger, +0.4; ale poured or a feast laid out, +0.3). A later slice adds its kinds by a line each (a debt defaulted, a hard deal at the fair, a breach of the gathering's peace) |
| Who knows it first | The parties, the adults of their households, and those at the occasion where it happened; what is said to all at a meeting or a rite, all who came |
| What a pawn keeps | At most 8 stories, each with the valence as it heard it, the number of mouths it came through, its strength and its zeal; a ninth takes the place of the one of least valence times strength. A story no living pawn holds is dropped from the state |
| Telling | In a talk each side tells a story at chance 0.3, times its sociability. It picks among those it holds by valence × zeal × strength, 1.3 times for bad news, more by the hearer's tie to the subject, against a weight of 1 for talk of other things. Nobody tells a pawn a story about itself but a close friend, which is how it learns what is said of it |
| Saturation | Telling a story to someone who already holds it halves the teller's zeal for it, and under 0.05 it tells it no more. Zeal is kept twice, for the teller's own village and for people from elsewhere, so old news is fresh at the gathering and at an ale evening |
| Sharpening | Each retelling makes the valence 8% stronger, with a little noise, within -1..1 |
| Belief | The hearer believes at chance 0.6, raised or lowered by half its trust in the teller less 0.5 (trust: its tie and its opinion of the teller) and by half the story's fit (its opinion of the subject, times the story's sign): it believes ill of those it dislikes |
| What a believed story does | The hearer holds it, and remembers the subject by it: a memory of what it has heard, of the told valence × 0.8 for each mouth it came through, at most 0.4 either way for one subject, fading as memories do. So a stranger's opinion is about a third of a witness's, and fades with the story |
| Forgetting | A story's strength halves every 40 game days untold or unheard (a first-hand one every 120), times 0.5 to 1.5 by the pawn's wits, and under 0.2 it is forgotten; telling or hearing it again restores it. Read in closed form when the story is next weighed, never by a daily pass |
| Lies | A pawn may invent a story about an enemy: at its talk turn, at chance 0.005 × (1 − its honour) × (0.5 + its ambition), of a kind above, and one lie about one pawn at a time. It then tells it as any story. The player's pawn may do the same by choice (the act Speak ill of, with whom and what), and is exposed as any liar is |
| Exposure | A lie told to a hearer with a first-hand memory of the subject, in the story's domain and of the other sign, is not believed: the hearer knows better. It then holds, first-hand, a counter-story about the one who made the lie up (a lie carries its author's name, as "Olof says the smith's iron is soft" does), of 1.5 times the lie's valence, times the hearer's honour (0.5 to 1.5) and its culture's mean honour (0.5 to 1.5); the teller is told it and stops telling the lie |
| Bands (target-v2, section 10.2; the measures are Kim's answers of 12 Nov) | A notable breach is held by over half of its village's adults within a season, and of the villages that share an ale evening or a gathering with it within a year; an island across water hears nothing until something joins them. Three years on, a story is held by under half of those who held it at its peak. A slander with a living witness reaches fewer than a true story of its kind, and its counter-story more than the lie. Stories do not polarise: under a quarter of held opinions at 0.8 or beyond, and no village with a fifth on each side of ±0.3 over one subject. The wrap-up fits belief (the most sensitive number) and the ale evening's draw from neighbouring villages, which is what carries news between villages |
| What the player hears | The pawn panel gains Heard: the stories that pawn holds, each a line saying whom it is about, what, from whom it was last heard, through how many mouths, and how well it is remembered. It is that pawn's belief and says so (Principles, The future is someone's estimate, applies to hearsay as to estimates): a lie is never marked as one, and an exposed lie shows as the counter-story. A story about the played pawn's own household that it comes to hear is a notable event, and stops Skip to the next event. The record and the History tabs show what happened, as before; stories are the first knowledge with a source, and the actor view still filters nothing else |

#### Courting (Decided, 30 October and 9 November: the shape; the numbers Agreed or Proposed as each says)

| Aspect | Design | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Starting | Two unmarried pawns of 16 or more, of opposite sex and no closer kin than the marriage limit, court once each holds the other at an opinion of 0.4 with a tie of 1.5: checked when the two have a good talk, never by a pass over pairs. A pawn courts one at a time; it is a life event of both | Agreed, 30 and 31 Oct (the gate measured: 16% of such pairs after two populous years) |
| Ending | When either's opinion of the other falls under 0.2, when one marries another or dies, or after 3 years unmarried | Proposed, 15 Nov |
| The wish at the round | A courting couple's wish is offered at the moving round before the matcher chooses, as the player's wish for a match is, if the round's rules allow it (ages 18 and 20, a place to live by the residence custom, the kin limit, the means, hunger under 0.5). It skips the matcher's lowest-score test, since their liking replaces it; those who speak for themselves need nothing more, and a spokesman answers by opinion as for any offer | Agreed, 9 Nov; the skipped test, Proposed (VB-233's plan) |
| A refusal | A couple whose head refuses keeps courting, and each remembers the head for it (as a refused match is remembered, -0.2). At the next round each may wish for service in another household (chance 0.5), where they may marry once they have a place | Proposed, 15 Nov |
| A child on the way | A courting woman may conceive, at a chance set so that about 5% of births come while courting (a starting value of 0.08 a year). When the pregnancy is known the couple's offer is made at once, out of the round, by the round's acts and under its rules. Whether they marry within the year is reported, not set (Kim's "about 3 in 4, if they can" is the band: 0.6 to 0.9) | Agreed, 30 Oct (5%; the band); the offer out of the round, Proposed (VB-233's plan) |
| A child outside marriage | Lives with its mother's household; the father is recorded and known. It inherits by the custom Children born outside marriage (Custom dimensions), and counts as born in marriage from the day its parents marry. Its birth is a story (kin) | Agreed, 30 Oct; legitimation by the parents' marriage, Proposed, 15 Nov |
| The pair's own offer | In place of "offered at the round before the matcher chooses", when the round concludes offers (Inheritance, The round as offers): a courting pair's wish is an offer of their own, made when the courtship starts and on each asking day after, due at the round, where it comes before any arranged one: the spokesman of a courting member arranges nothing else. A courting pawn says no to a match arranged for it, unless it holds its spokesman dearer than the one it courts; then it yields, and remembers (-0.3, 5 years). A couple whose head refused ranks service above staying at the next round when each holds that head at an opinion under 0 (it was a chance of 0.5) | Proposed, 16 Nov (VB-682's plan; VB-036, rewritten) |

#### Cost, named before building (rule 9), at about 1,000 living

Talk and contacts take 55.3 ms a game day at `medium-ref` (the bench of 10 November), against a share of 55; the slice must end
under where it starts. On populous small, year 1, the hourly round over all the living and the search for partners were 48% of talk's
time (a profile of 15 November), and both go with the number of pawns asked.

| System | Entities × cadence × work | Expected |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The talk round, by turns | 1,000 pawns × 4 turns a day (it was 16 hours) × up to 8 partners weighed; about 4,200 talks a day (it was 6,600) | Talk's share down by a quarter to a third (the trial's profile: 7.7% of the run to 5.2%, with the round still walking everyone; counters `talkTurn` 4 a pawn-day, `talkWeighed` 6.5 to 6.2) |
| Chemistry | 12 numbers and a byte a custom per pawn, read where compatibility is read today; nothing stored per pair | No new loop |
| Traits | Written at birth and at 15 (about 40 births a year); read as an array index; 12 numbers a pawn in the save, about 0.1 MB of JSON | Nothing daily |
| Stories | 4,200 talks × 2 sides × 0.3 × up to 8 stories weighed, about 20,000 weights a day; a hearer does one lookup, and a write only when it first believes; at most 8 held a pawn (8,000, about 0.4 MB of JSON), and the live stories are those someone holds | About 2 ms a day, inside the saving |
| Occasions as venues | At most one ale evening a village a day × up to 20 guests; 3 rites a year × villages × those who come; partners sampled, never paired | Under 0.5 ms a day |
| Courting | A check at a good talk of two young unmarried pawns; conception in the births step that already walks women | No new loop |
| Mood | Read when a panel is open, and once at each allowance weighed (a gift, an ask, help: about 400 a year at populous small), without allocating | Nothing a step |

The slice claims nothing from the budget's rest: talk's share stays 55 ms and the wrap-up (VB-063) reports the share at the slice's
start and end on `medium-ref` and `small-y40`.

### Marriage, nursing and growth (the plan of VB-405, 16 November)

What holds the population is marriage, and what a couple waits for is modelled, never the age at which it may marry (Vision and scope,
Principles: mechanisms, not prescriptions). Kim's answers of 13 November (`QUESTION_ARCHIVE.md`, "VB-405") are the Decided rows; the
rest is the plan's, decided by the principles. The budget these rows are measured against is in Balance loops and targets (The growth
budget); the rules of the round and of the offer are in Inheritance (Leaving home; Marriage as a negotiation) and stay as they are.

| Aspect | Design | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The gate | A couple marries when four things hold at the moving round: a place to live under the residence custom, kin no closer than the custom's limit, the means in the home they will live in, and neither at a hunger of 0.5 or more. Nothing else holds a marriage back, and nothing sets an age: 18 for women and 20 for men are the least ages, a rule of the pawns that a custom may one day hold, and not what the valve moves. The gate stays as built (VB-153) | Decided, 13 Nov (Kim: keep the gate, add nothing that sets an age) |
| The setpoint: the means to marry | The home holds food above its seed for `MARRIAGE.means` years (1.5) of its members and the newcomer, counting grain, grain in sheaf, meals and the larder; a home with fewer than two adults is exempt, since it needs the spouse's hands. It is the valve's setpoint: a bad harvest lowers what homes hold and marriages fall the same autumn, and the value sets how much food a head the island keeps late in the game (in the review's mock 10% more raised the late food ratio by 4.7%, and land concentration, the town's food and hunger follow that ratio). It is a rule of the registry (`marriage.means`), so that a custom, a head's caution or a later slice can move it as a contribution with its cause, and so that it is explained where it binds: the pawn panel says "cannot marry yet: the home holds 0.9 of the 1.5 years it needs", which is what the home holds today, counted, and no forecast. Its value is moved only in a tuning run of Kim's | Decided, 13 Nov (the setpoint); the rule and its words, VB-405's plan (VB-631) |
| What moves the age | Four waits, each a mechanism. **A holding:** under the residence custom "with whoever holds land" one of the two needs a place (a farm held, the first place in line for one, a cottage on a plot, a farm on new land), and the queue for land grows when the land within a day's walk of a village is taken (measured on populous small: adults waiting for land rose from 3 to 47 in 50 years, and marriages with no land within reach were 62 to 74 a decade). **The means,** above: a year's wait after a poor harvest (marriages put off rose from none to 52 to 122 a decade). **Provisions:** a party for new land needs 42 days of rations and seed for a field (Moving to new land). **Service,** below. Courting adds none of its own: a courting couple waits on the same gate (Traits, talk and stories: Courting). The age at first marriage by sex is reported and never set (measured: women 20 to 21, men 22 to 23, while land is free) | Decided, 13 Nov (the age rises by itself as homes grow scarce); the list, VB-405's plan |
| Never marrying | The share who reach 45 unmarried is what the gate leaves over: those who never came to a place. It is reported and has no target or quota (history: a quarter to a third in north-west Europe; measured on populous small in 50 years: almost nobody, since land is still free) | Decided, 13 Nov (not set); VB-405's plan |
| Service and marriage | Service is by the year, from one moving round to the next (the servants' moving day, which the round is). A servant's home is the master's, and it is no home to bring a spouse to under any residence custom; a servant does not marry within the year, so an offer out of the round (A child on the way) waits for the round; and at the round a servant marries by leaving service, for the spouse's place or one of its own. A village short of land has more hands a field, so more of its young go into service, to wherever hands are short (Leaving home, Service), and the years they serve are years without a holding: more of them marry late, and some never. No share of servants is prescribed | Decided, 13 Nov (Kim: a servant does not marry while in service); the year and the home, VB-405's plan (VB-634) |
| The portion | What a servant earns is its road to a holding. Proposed, for when wages exist as obligations (slice 5; Exchange, Obligations): a servant is owed `SERVICE.wage` (12 rations of grain) for a year served, at the round that ends it, from the master's grain above its reserve, as a sibling's share is owed; it lies in the master's store until it is fetched, and it counts as the servant's stake, toward the provisions of a party for new land and the means of the home it marries into. A couple with nothing from home then saves the 84 days of rations and the seed in about four years of two wages, which is the wait history shows. It replaces "board and lodging only" (Agreed, 30 Oct) when it is built | Proposed, 16 Nov (VB-637) |
| Nursing | A mother does not conceive while she nurses, for `BIRTH.nursingDays` (48 days, a year). The child's death ends the nursing, so a family that loses an infant has the next child sooner: the part of the birth interval that regulates itself, worth about half a point of yearly growth in the review's mock where a fifth of children die before 1. The length stays fixed until a custom of it is wanted. The pawn panel says that a mother nurses, and whom | Decided, 13 Nov (Kim); VB-632 |
| Left out, and why | Miscarriage and stillbirth: the birth numbers are live births, calibrated as such, and a lost pregnancy gives neither the head nor the player a decision. Deaths by season: they wait for disease (slice 8). Fertility chosen within marriage: not modelled; the valve is marriage | VB-405's plan, by the principle of detail |
| A chance that is no mechanism | `MARRIAGE.goAhead` (0.75), the chance that a match the round found goes ahead that year, stands in for a judgement of the heads that is not modelled. It delays a marriage by a third of a year on average and answers to nothing. It stays until the heads answer by opinion and courting has landed (VB-275, VB-036); the wrap-up (VB-638) reports the delay it adds and proposes its end | VB-405's plan |
| Founders | The start stays as Decided and as built: ten unrelated founders, four couples and two servants, so six lines, and the kin limit at first cousins. What holds a founders' island is the size of its pool, not the gate. Measured (16 Nov; founders small, seeds 3, 7, 11 and 42, 80 years): yearly growth 1.1%, 0, 2.6% and 2.2% (the nightly band's sixteen seeds over 50 years: a median of 2.2%, 12 Nov); of the years the unmarried of age under 45 waited, 47% had nobody unmarried of the other sex on the island, 22% only kin too close, 6% only matches the score refuses, 1% no home, and none failed the means; women first married at 20 to 22. A seed whose pool closes stands still (seed 7: ten living at year 80, every unmarried adult too close kin to every other), and may die out; that is accepted (Growth: not every seed need grow). What a task may still try is the founders' ages, since younger couples give a larger and closer-aged second generation (VB-635) | Decided (the start; Kim, 13 Nov: four unrelated families may stand); measured and VB-405's plan, 16 Nov |
| Settling families | None before the ship. With the ship (slice 8) a family of another culture may land at the harbour and walk to a village that has a plot or an empty farm for it, with what it brought in the ship; the plan of slice 8 sets how many and how often, from the proposal of one family in 2 years while fewer than 300 live and one in 10 years after (VB-636). Nobody appears on the island or in a village without having travelled there | Decided, 13 Nov (Kim); the numbers Proposed, for VB-188 to confirm |

## 5. Inheritance, expansion and other bodies

A couple needs a household of its own to marry, and one heir takes each farm, so younger siblings become landless adults who must serve, marry an heir or a widow, claim an empty farm, or clear new land.

This follows the Northwestern European pattern of household formation (Hajnal 1965). All rules below read the village's customs. (Agreed)

### Succession

1. **The surviving spouse** first, as the widow custom allows: keeps the farm for life, keeps only her keep, or inherits outright.
2. **Children** in the order the sex and birth-order customs give; a dead child's children stand in their place.
3. **The holder's siblings** and their lines, then the parents' siblings (cousins) and theirs.

- Someone who already holds a farm, or lives on a spouse's farm, is passed over.
- Someone first in line for their own parents' farm steps behind the other relatives: farms are not combined while someone else can take them. This is a custom (Decided; see Custom dimensions). Under "keeps their place", an heir first in two lines takes the farm with more fields and drops the other claim, which passes to the next in that line, so no household holds two farms (Decided, 30 Oct).
- A minor heir holds in name, with a guardian (*förmyndare*) until 18: the surviving parent or the nearest adult relative.
- No heir: the farm stands empty and the village holds it, owning its house and field tiles (Decided). Historically, heirless property (*danaarv*) went to the Crown.
- **Handed on, and taken up** (Proposed, VB-682's plan, 16 Nov; VB-824). In life the holder offers the holding to the first in line, at the time the custom gives (a played holder when it chooses), for a keep on the farm for life; the heir answers, and says no only when it holds or is betrothed to a place with more fields, which passes the offer to the next in line. At a death the inheritance falls at once to the first in line, as a right does, and the heir takes it up by walking there or renounces it for a better place. The order and the passing over above are unchanged: they are what the answers give.

### Handover and shares

- **Timing** is the holder's choice (Decided, Kim, 18 Nov; was: follows the custom): it hands on in life when it chooses, the player for its pawn as every holder for itself, else the farm passes at its death. The custom (when the heir marries, the parents retiring on the farm, *undantag*; or when the holder turns 60) is when the village expects it. An unplayed holder weighs the custom's time, its years and strength, the heir's wish to marry, its opinion of the heir and the keep the farm can bear (`HANDOVER`, Proposed, VB-1122's plan, 19 Nov; built by VB-1314, under every custom, 'death' having no custom's time); a holder who holds out past the custom's time loses its heir's opinion, a little a year (`HANDOVER.grudge`), derived from `household.expected`.
- **Shares:** under “one heir buys out the rest” each sibling receives a share of the farm's value; under *systerlott* a daughter gets half a son's share. Shares can be paid partly in animals.
- **A reserve** of seed, food and winter hay is never paid out; any shortfall is recorded as owed.
- **Shares still owed** are paid by the farm the sibling came from, at each moving round, from grain above its reserve; the shares become the new household's starting grain (Agreed, 30 Oct).
- **Splitting land** between heirs is designed as a custom, but behaves like a buy-out until splitting exists. A sibling who gets fields also needs a house next to them; until it is built, the old household works those fields and gives the sibling half the harvest (Agreed, 30 Oct).

### The retired (undantag) (VB-1122's plan, 19 November)

A holder who hands on retires on the farm with a keep, by the Nordic retirement contract (*undantag*, *födoråd*). What follows is every
retired pawn's, played or not (principle 16); `docs/plans/VB-1122.md` gives the reasons and the numbers.

| Aspect | Design | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The keep | Named at the handover: a room, one adult's rations, firewood (`KEEP.wood`) and, where the farm has cows, one cow's milk; an obligation (kind `keep`) of the new holder's household to the retired pawn, from the handover to its death, met at the table while it lives in the house | Proposed, VB-1122's plan, 19 Nov |
| The keep short | A day the retired goes hungry while the store held its ration is a day short; `KEEP.shortDays` in a year press the keep: a memory of the holder ("Kept me short") and a case at the next meeting, as a pressed debt's; pressed `KEEP.reclaim` years running, the meeting may give the farm back to its old holder, as the provincial laws allowed | Proposed, VB-1122's plan, 19 Nov |
| A voice with the heir | Once a year, at the head's spring planning day, the retired may advise the head on the plan (a target, the rotation, a crop out of turn): an offer of basis `advice`, which the head takes when its opinion of the adviser times the adviser's standing reaches `ADVICE.heed`, and then carries out itself. An unplayed retired advises the custom's hay where its head's departs by more than `ADVICE.gap` (0.1); `ADVICE.heed` 0.2, so a parent on ordinary terms is heeded, one at odds or of low standing is not | Built (VB-1316); a played retired pawn's sheet of its decisions waits for VB-1484 |
| At the meeting | As every adult, under "Who speaks at the meeting"; a cause argued as any pawn's (`argue`) | Proposed, VB-1122's plan, 19 Nov (as built) |
| A grandchild's match | Under "Who makes a match" by the heads, a retired grandparent in the house may wish a match for a grandchild of age, carried to the head as advice; the partner the one who seeks in its village it thinks best of | Built (VB-1316) |
| Gifts from its own | What its keep names is its own to give (the milk of its cow, rations it saved), by `give`, from the keep's reservation in the household's store | Proposed, VB-1122's plan, 19 Nov; waits for the keep |
| Work | By the role `retired`'s weights, and any open job of its household, as built | Agreed (as built) |
| The player retired | Every year at least the spring advice and the autumn meeting; a grandchild's match, a keep pressed, a gift when they come. A year with nothing taken is skipped to the next thing asked of the pawn | Proposed, VB-1122's plan, 19 Nov |

### Leaving home

- **The moving round** follows the meetings each autumn: handovers and a fresh count of who wants to move out. It is also when households negotiate marriages: each match the round finds is an offer both households answer, made in the round of the man's village (Marriage as a negotiation, below; Decided, 9 Nov; the offer Agreed, 9 Nov). (Was, Decided 30 Oct: marriages come mostly from courting, at any time of year, and the yearly matchmaking stays for pawns who are not courting, as arranged marriages.)
- **A village's round** (Decided, 10 Nov; Kim, code review: the game is about locally supported community). Every village holds its own round on autumn day 11, as today, each in its own six-minute step from noon, in the order of village id. A lone farm is a village of one farm and holds its own. A village's round matches its own unmarried men, by today's score, and a woman of any village may be chosen; the same-village bonus stands. A woman married in an earlier village's round, or held by an open offer from it, is no longer free. Its handovers follow, for its own farms and for the homes in other villages that its marriages chose, and so does its count of those who found no spouse only because of kin. The order changes who asks first. The whole island's men used to ask in one list, those with a place first, then the oldest. Now every man of village 0 asks before any man of village 1, so a woman of a later village may be chosen by an earlier village's man before her own village's men ask. Measured, the first third of villages' men lose the woman they score best about half as often as before (backlog VB-242). So the starting village turns by the year: the round starts at village `year mod count` and goes on in id order, wrapping, and the meetings with it (Decided, Kim, 10 Nov; VB-280 builds it). Who wants to move out, claims on empty farms, plots, settlement and service follow once every village has held its round, over the whole island as before (Proposed, VB-242; tasks VB-277, VB-279); since 10 November the distance they reach is walking hours (Between villages, below; Proposed, VB-281, `QUESTIONS.md`, VB-281, question 2).
- **Marriage** is negotiated between the two households (Decided, 9 Nov; was: comes from courting, at any time of year). It needs a place to live under the residence custom, kin no closer than the custom's limit, by civil degree, and the means to marry in the home. Matches favour the same village and an age gap of about 3 years; since 10 November, villages near by walking hours, and no woman beyond walking reach (Between villages, Ease of mixing; Proposed, VB-281). The two marry when both and their spokesmen have said yes: at once when no player answers, else within 7 days of the round (Marriage as a negotiation, The offer).
- **Wants to move out:** an adult of 18 or more with no farm, no spouse with a farm, and no place in line for one.
- **Service:** a landless adult may take service where labour is short: below 1 hand (members 14–59, not retired) per field. At the moving round unmarried landless adults from households above 1.5 hands per field go, oldest first, to the shortest household of their village, else of the nearest village within 15 steps; board and lodging only (backlog VB-090; Agreed, 30 Oct). Proposed, VB-281: the nearest village within a day's walk (12 h) in place of 15 steps, and the servant walks there (Between villages).
- **A start in life:** a cow and a few ewes from the family's share give a new household its start.

### The round as offers (the plan of VB-682, 16 November; Proposed)

Every act is a pawn's (Principles for decisions), and the round was the actor: its matcher paired the unmarried and its steps handed out farms, plots, places in service and new land. Kim's answers of 16 November turn it round: offers are made all the year and the round is their due date; a move needs the pawn's yes and the receiving head's, the head left only for a marriage or one under age; heads arrange matches and the member may refuse; and a pawn pursues one rung at a time, a rung being one answer to "where do I live next year". The plan is `docs/plans/VB-682.md`; the rows are Proposed until their tasks land, and until then the rules above (Leaving home) and below (Marriage as a negotiation, Households short of people, Moving to new land) are the game's. Where a row moves a Decided point it says so, and Kim may strike it.

| Aspect | Design | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The offer | One shape for every crossing between households (Exchange, The gift; VB-690): from one pawn to another, on a basis, with what is given and wanted and when it is due. Six bases are about a life: a match, a place in service, kin care, land (an empty farm or a plot), a party for new land, a handover. The one asked answers once, when the offer is due: the round for a life, at once for care, for a match at a gathering both attend, and for a child on the way | Proposed, 16 Nov (Kim: offers all the year, the round their due date) |
| The rungs | Heir to the farm at home; marry onto a holding; an empty farm; a plot; new land; a place in service; stay at home. A rung is open by a fact (a partner within reach, a farm the custom lets it claim, a plot site, a crowded village and the rounds it has waited there, hands to spare at home), and a pawn's wish ranks them: in this order by default, shifted by ambition (new land opens after 3 rounds of waiting at ambition 0, 2 at 0.5 and 1 at 1, and ranks above a plot from 0.75), by courting (its match is the one it courts), by a head's refusal (service before staying) and by hunger (not this year). It holds one open offer about its own future, and makes the next when that is refused or lapses. The played pawn sets its own ranking | Proposed, 16 Nov (Kim: one rung at a time; the ranking is the plan's, VB-680) |
| The asking day | A pawn who seeks (unmarried and of age, or grown and landless) reviews its wish once a season, on a day of its own, and if it holds no open offer it makes one; a son or daughter of the house has the match made by its spokesman. Occasions make offers on their own days: the start of a courtship, a suitor's arrival, a gathering. Nobody is asked by more than 5 open offers on one basis, and a spokesman weighs at most 40 candidates, the nearest villages first | Proposed, 16 Nov (VB-823) |
| The round concludes | The round chooses nobody: it answers what is due and makes the moves, each a journey. It lasts three days, autumn days 11 to 13 (**moves** A village's round, Decided 10 Nov: one day), each village in its step as before and the starting village turning by the year. A village's step answers the offers addressed to its people (**moves** the same row: the man's village matched its men), in the order handover, match, land, party, place, so that one yes is known to the next; a pawn takes part only in the step of the offer it holds. One refused on a day makes its next offer that evening (a match: the next best within reach, the one who refused left out; never for the played pawn, never on the last day); what is open after the third day lapses. As built, the round's choosers (match, claims, plots, settlers, service) ask on the first day only, and the second and third days answer only the offers due on them (Proposed, VB-692, 17 Nov). History: the servants' free week at the autumn moving day | Proposed, 16 Nov (VB-692) |
| Whose yes | The pawn's own and the receiving head's, always; the head left only for a marriage, as spokesman where the custom gives him a voice, or for one under 18. A minor moves with the grown pawn who keeps it, or under an offer of care its kin answered. An adult leaves for service or new land without leave | Decided, 16 Nov (Kim) |
| A match | The spokesman of one who wishes to marry asks the best-scoring partner the gate allows among the unmarried within a day's walk, of a village visited since the last round and of his last gathering (Between villages). The score is the round's (the gap of their ages, the walking hours, the two's opinions, a fancy drawn once at the asking), and gains 0.5 a point of the spokesman's opinion of the other spokesman. The man's side asks; a woman's side that holds no offer asks from the summer, and a woman who holds a farm at once. At the round her side answers the best offer it holds and refuses the rest: a spokesman weighs the score as he reads it, the means of the home and, later, the terms; one to marry says no at enmity, while courting another, or under "not this year". The gate (a place, the kin limit, the means, no hunger: People, Marriage, nursing and growth) is not moved and is read at the round. The go-ahead roll of 0.75 goes. Remembered: a member who refused its head's match (as before); a head who refused a courting pair; a spokesman whose offer was refused other than for a better one (-0.1, 2 years); a spokesman by the member who yielded (-0.3, 5 years) | Proposed, 16 Nov (Kim: heads arrange, the member may refuse, opinion weighs and follows; the numbers are the plan's, VB-693) |
| A betrothal | A match whose home is a place still asked for (a farm claimed, a plot, new land) is answered as any; agreed, the two are betrothed: held for each other, counted as a couple in the custom's order for land, and married at the first round after the place is theirs, if the gate still holds. It lapses at the third round | Proposed, 16 Nov (Kim: a match may ride on a place rung; VB-825) |
| A place in service | A head whose household is short of hands, or who lives alone, offers a place to one adult: kin first, then by his opinion, then the nearer, within a day's walk. Or the adult whose best open rung is service asks such a head. The adult says no at enmity, when its home would be left short without it, or when the master's house is hungrier than its own; the head says yes while still short. Service is by the year (People, Service and marriage): at the round a servant stays unasked unless a better rung is open to it; nobody is dismissed. It replaces "oldest first, to the shortest household" (Agreed, 30 Oct); the hands a field still decide who serves | Proposed, 16 Nov (VB-694) |
| Land | A landless adult, or a betrothed pair, asks the alderman of the village (until there is one, its eldest living holder; in a village with no holder the asker's own word stands: Proposed, VB-692, 17 Nov) for a named empty farm or for a plot; among several the custom's order decides, as before, and the alderman adds no judgement of his own. Joining a small village is asking its alderman for a plot there (**moves** Moving to new land, Where: a party joined unasked; the distances stand) | Proposed, 16 Nov (VB-697) |
| New land | One whose wish reaches new land calls a party: its spouse, and at most 2 unmarried siblings waiting in the village. The spouse says yes unless she or a child she keeps is hungry (0.5 or more); the provisions are counted at the move (Provisions, below), not at the call; a sibling says yes when it holds no offer of its own and does not think ill of the caller. This replaces the chance to leave (0.3, 0.6, 0.8) and the chance to follow (0.5) of Moving to new land, Deciding (Agreed, 2 Nov). The call says only who goes, and is made and answered at step 1 of Settling in steps (Moving to new land; VB-445), whose "leader found as before" it replaces: the site, the trail, the booth and the move are that plan's, and the party's people change household at its step 4, the move | Proposed, 16 Nov (Kim: nobody is drawn; VB-696) |
| One move | Every change of household is one act, `move`, with its reason (marriage, heir, service, guardian, care, home, settle, found) and the minors who follow (the actor's or its spouse's minor children, or its ward; a family moves as two moves, the lead and then the spouse with the children; the reason is kept on the record's entry of the move, and the verb `serve` is gone into it: Proposed, VB-822, 17 Nov): one place for whose yes it needs, for the journey and its provisions, and for the event. Service, the guardian's coming, kin taken in and the people of a dissolved household are a move and nothing more. A marriage, a handover, a trust and the founding of a household keep their own acts, since each also changes a right, which moves at once and where it lies while the person walks; each makes its people's moves | Proposed, 16 Nov (VB-822) |
| Cost | No pass over pairs: an asking is a due entry a seeker a season (about 60 to 120 seekers a 1,000 living), weighing at most 40 candidates within a day's walk; the round answers about one open offer a seeker, over three days, a village a step. The work grows with the seekers, not with men times women as the matcher's did | Proposed, 16 Nov |

### Marriage as a negotiation (9 November)

From the gameplay review of 9 November. A peasant marriage between landholding families was a settlement between two households, of people, land and goods, and it is the largest choice of a life; so it is a negotiation the player takes part in, not a match the round makes over its head. The first row is Kim's; the rest was proposed with it and accepted the same day (`QUESTION_ARCHIVE.md`, "VB-233"). VB-233's plan (10 Nov) checked them against the code and corrected two rows to the form the round can carry, marked below; its tasks are VB-293 (offers), VB-273 (the offer in the page), VB-274 (the wish for a match), VB-275 (opinion; answered by Kim, 10 Nov, and built 11 Nov) and VB-276 (terms, slice 4).

| Aspect | Design | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The principle | A marriage is negotiated between two households, and the player takes part: for its own pawn, and as head of a household for the household's members | Decided, 9 Nov |
| Who negotiates | Each side's spokesman (*giftoman*): the head of the household the pawn lives in as a son or daughter of the house; an adult with no such head (a holder, a widow or widower, a servant, a cottager) speaks for itself. A son or daughter of the house is a child of the holder, of the holder's spouse or of the holder's parent who lives in the household and is not in service (VB-233) | Agreed, 9 Nov |
| Consent | Both who are to marry must consent, as canon law asked; a pawn that refuses a match its head made loses some of the head's opinion (a memory, "Refused the match I made", -0.2 for 3 years). In the first version only the player refuses, so only the player's refusal costs it; where opinion enters the answers of pawns without a player is open (`QUESTIONS.md`, VB-275). A cause for a custom ("Who makes a match": the heads; the heads with consent; the couple) is a candidate | Agreed, 9 Nov; the memory's size Proposed (VB-233) |
| The offer | First version: the round's matcher finds the pairs it finds today, in the round of the man's village, and each becomes an offer that both sides answer; a side with no player answers yes, so a run with no player is unchanged. An offer the player must answer is kept as plain data with its due time, the round goes on without it, and the two are not free to anyone else while it is open, a later village's round of the same day included (VB-242); it closes with the marriage (and the handover a marriage brings) when all have said yes, refused at the first no, or at its due time, when those who have not answered answer as the pawn would unplayed (Proposed, VB-690, 17 Nov; it was: say yes). A refused match waits for the next round: the round does not look again for the two that year. The spouse whose new home lies in another village walks there after the marriage (Between villages, A journey; Proposed, VB-281). Later, heads look for matches themselves and weigh the other household's land, stores, standing and the tie between the two | Agreed, 9 Nov; corrected 10 Nov (VB-233; accepted by Kim, 16 Nov): the round marries as it goes and each marriage changes the homes and heirs the next choice reads, so an offer cannot hold the round open; it is answered after it |
| Terms | First version: none beyond the means to marry and A start in life, above. With slice 4's goods and worth: a dowry (*hemföljd*), counted against the bride's share of the inheritance, and a morning gift (*morgongåva*) from the groom's side, hers if she is widowed; both booked in customary worth | Agreed, 9 Nov |
| Courting | Keeps its place from slice 7: a courting couple's wish is what moves their heads to negotiate, and is enough by itself for those who speak for themselves; a courted match the heads refuse may end in a birth outside marriage, or in the couple leaving for service. The negotiation is the round's offer: a courting couple's wish works as the player's wish for a match does, for both of them (backlog VB-036 writes it) | Agreed, 9 Nov |
| The player's doors | As the one to marry: a wish for a match (carried to the round as its other wishes are; the wish is any pawn's, the player's being its pawn's: Proposed, VB-680, 17 Nov) and yes or no to an offer. The wish is "not this year", which keeps the pawn out of the round's matching, or a partner, offered at the man's village's round before the matcher chooses, if the round's rules allow the pair (age, kin, a home with the means to marry) and its score, with chance at its mean, reaches the round's lowest accepted score; the wish is the pawn's own yes. As head: yes or no for a member, and, later (with the terms, slice 4), proposing a match and its terms. An offer is the first question asked through `Decisions.answer`; it stays open for 7 game days, the world does not wait, and unanswered the pawn answers as it would unplayed | Agreed, 9 Nov; the wish's form Agreed (VB-233; accepted by Kim, 16 Nov: a wished partner must pass the round's rules and its lowest score, which stands for the other side's judgement until opinion enters) |
| Offers through the year | Kim's answers of 16 November: offers are made all the year and the round is their due date; heads arrange and the member may refuse. It rewrites the rows The offer, Courting and The player's doors when its tasks land, and is told in The round as offers, above: a spokesman asks on the member's asking day, her side answers the best offer it holds at the round, the four who answer weigh the score, opinion and the means, the go-ahead roll goes, and a refusal is remembered on both sides. A custom, Who makes a match (the heads; the heads with consent, the default; the couple), says whose voice counts; it was named a candidate in the Consent row | Proposed, 16 Nov (VB-682's plan; VB-693, VB-826) |

### Between villages: distance and journeys (10 November)

Kim's decision of 10 November (`QUESTION_ARCHIVE.md`, "VB-242", question 2) on what reaches between villages: a wife from another village, claims on empty farms, settlement, service. Until now each reached by its own measure, hex steps as the crow flies or nothing, and nobody walked: a bride married into a village 60 hours away stood at her husband's door in the same six-minute step. VB-281's plan (survey and numbers: `backlog/VB-281.md`) proposes the rows below; its open forks are in `QUESTION_ARCHIVE.md`, "VB-281", and its tasks are VB-286 (the distance), VB-287 (journeys), VB-288 (the matching), VB-289 (the suitor's visit), VB-290 (the round's other acts), VB-291 (kin help) and VB-292 (the player's journey).

| Aspect | Design | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The principle | "It should be governed by intra village distance, there should be easy mixing for close villages, but preparing for a long trip is not cheap. The pawn should physically travel." (Kim; "intra village" read as between villages) | Decided, 10 Nov |
| Distance | One measure between two villages: the walking hours between their greens, by the route search's costs (terrain, slope, worn paths) at 8 tiles an hour on meadow (World and scale, The journey scale: 3 until 12 November, then 12; the fixtures' hours quoted in this table were measured at 3 and are not measured again here). The thresholds keep their hours at every speed, since each is argued from the walker's day (VB-444's plan, 16 Nov). Compiled into a table, a village's row a step on autumn day 9 (so at most a year old) and at a village's founding, bounded at the route search's reach, 67 hours (536 meadow steps at 8 tiles an hour; the bound is kept in hours, `ROUTE.hours`, where it was 200 steps whatever the speed); beyond it, or with no route by land, a pair is out of walking reach. At `medium-ref` (29 villages) the median pair is 41 hours apart and a village's nearest neighbour 3.6 to 16 (median 5.2); at `small-y40` (23 villages) 27 hours, and 3.6 to 9.6 (median 6.2). This is the "one reach query" of Climate and travel beyond walking, on foot; boats, carts and ice add to it in slice 10 | Proposed (VB-286) |
| Near and far | A day's walk is 12 hours, 6:00 to 18:00. **There and back in a day:** 6 hours or less; work done from home reaches this far (VB-281 counts 14 pairs at `medium-ref`, 6 at `small-y40`). **A day's walk:** 12 hours or less; villages this close mix as neighbours (52 pairs, a median of 4 neighbours a village, at `medium-ref`; 38 and 3 at `small-y40`). **A long trip:** more than 12 hours, nights on the road. **Out of reach:** no route by land, or more than 67 hours. At the journey scale (populous medium, year 0, seeds 3, 7, 11 and 42) the median village has 1 or 2 villages within 6 hours, 2 to 4 within a day's walk and 6 to 13 within 24 hours; one or two villages an island have none within a day's walk, and on three islands of four 16 to 37 pairs by land are out of reach | Proposed; the hours kept and the counts at the journey scale, VB-444's plan, 16 Nov |
| Ease of mixing | The round's choice of a wife weighs the walking hours between the two villages: its village term is 2 × exp(-hours / 8), so 2 within the village, as before, 1.2 at 4 hours, 0.45 at 12, 0.1 at 24 and nothing at two days; it replaces the term of the two villages' ties to their culture's centre. A woman out of walking reach is not in the pool: a village's pool is the living of the villages within reach (`MARRIAGE.nearHours` 8 is the scale). The played pawn's wished partner is read wherever it lives, and a partner beyond reach is refused with that reason; every other pawn's wish names a partner only from its own choosing, within its pool (Proposed, VB-288, 17 Nov). In the fixtures' last ten years, 56 of 118 marriages at `medium-ref` joined villages more than a day's walk apart or over the sea. At the journey scale the nearest neighbour, 2.6 to 2.9 hours off, weighs 1.4, and all of a village's other villages together about half of what they did at 12 tiles an hour (a sum of exp(-hours / 8) of 1.8 to 2.9 at the median village of populous medium, from 3.0 to 6.3): marriage turns to the village and its neighbour. The scale of 8 hours stands and waits for the tuning run (`docs/tuning.md`) | Proposed (VB-288); the journey scale's figures, VB-444's plan, 16 Nov |
| The suitor's visit | A woman of a village beyond a day's walk may be chosen only by a man who visited her village since the last round. A man who found no one at his village's round, and whose home has the means to marry and the visit's food, sets out in winter for the nearest village beyond a day's walk that holds a free woman he could marry; he stays a day as a guest and walks home. | Decided, 10 Nov (Kim) (VB-289) |
| A journey | A move within a village is walked too, as a walk home to the new door and no journey (Agreed, 10 Nov; VB-287). A pawn whose new home lies in another village walks there: its household changes at the act (the marriage, the service, the claim), and the pawn walks the route from where it stands, a leg a day from 6:00 to 18:00, sleeping where it stops, takes no work on the road, and arrives. Each leg is a due entry, so nothing is polled; the walk is the simulation's whether or not the camera sees it | Proposed (VB-287) |
| Provisions and the season | A pawn on the road eats food carried from the household it leaves (not from the one it joins, which gives nothing until it is there: with no household to leave, it goes unprovisioned; Proposed, VB-676, 17 Nov; it was: or, short of it, from the one it joins), a ration a day of walking, and is left out of the household's meals meanwhile; settlers walk on their provisions. A visit costs its household a ration a day away and the visitor's hands: 2 × ceil(hours / 12) + 1 days, so 5 days for a village 13 to 24 hours away, 9 for 41 hours, 13 for 67. Moves walk in any season, as the act falls; a visit is made in winter, the farm's slack season, on the stored food that makes it dear | Proposed (VB-287, VB-289) |
| Who travels | Before the act: the suitor's visit. After it, to the new home: the spouse who moves, a servant, a guardian and its family, children sent to kin or service, a claimant's household when its farm is mended, and a settlers' party to its site or the small village it joins. Work done away from home (a claimant mending a farm, a plot) is walked to daily as any job is, within its reach | Proposed |
| The round's other acts | Claims on empty farms from villages within 6 hours (there and back in a day: the claimant mends the farm from home), service within a day's walk (12 hours), an orphan's service home in the nearest village by walking hours, guardians and kin homes within walking reach, the tie to the culture's centre as exp(-hours / 8); today's 15 hex steps measured 3.6 to 10.8 hours. They stay island-wide steps after the villages' rounds (open: `QUESTIONS.md`, "VB-281", question 2). Settlement's sites stay a search of tiles from the green | Decided, 10 Nov (Kim) (VB-290) |
| Kin help | Kin within a day's walk give as now; then the village store; then kin further away, whose gift one of their adults carries on a journey (at most a load: 30 rations on the back, 90 with a cart and two oxen, one number with the fair's; Kim, 10 Nov, was 60) and which arrives with him. Of one year's kin gifts, 34 of 116 at `medium-ref` and 49 of 80 at `small-y40` came from beyond a day's walk or over the sea. Near kin's gift and the store's are not brought: the hungry household fetches them (Goods and the farming year, Help in hunger; Proposed, VB-461, 17 Nov) | Decided, 10 Nov (Kim) (VB-291) |
| Over the sea | No marriage, claim, service or help between villages with no route by land until slice 10's boats and ice (Climate and travel beyond walking); every village of both fixtures has a neighbour by land within 16 hours. | Decided, 10 Nov (Kim) |
| The player's journey | By parity of command, the played pawn, or as head an unmarried son or daughter of the house, may set out on a visit at the same cost and season. | Decided, 10 Nov (Kim) (VB-292) |
| Cost | Village pairs are few (406 at `medium-ref`): the table holds villages² hours, a row a step (a bounded search from the green, 2.4 ms median and 19 ms at most at `medium-ref`; the table 95 ms a year, spread). About 26 journeys a year at `medium-ref` and 10 at `small-y40`, each one route search and a due entry a leg. The pool of the round shrinks to the women within reach, but fewer pairs are scored only where reach cuts, by the sea or beyond 67 hours: at `small-y40`, where every pair by land lies within 16.3 hours, a year's pairs are 110 before and after; what brings the pairs down is the suitor's visit's day's walk (VB-289) | Proposed; the pairs as measured, Proposed (VB-288, 17 Nov) |

### Empty farms

- **Buildings** lose about 8% of their condition a year; below 40% the turf roof has fallen in, below 15% the farm is a ruin to rebuild.
- **Fields** turn to meadow within 1–2 years; near woodland, without grazing or farming, meadow goes on to scrub and young forest. Each stage needs a longer clearing job.
- **Claims** follow the claims custom, married couples before singles. A claim posts restoration jobs owned by the claimant's household; they move in once the roof is repaired. Agreed. Proposed, VB-281: a claimant from another village must live within 6 walking hours of the farm's village, in place of 15 hex steps (Between villages, The round's other acts).
- **Restoration hours** grow as the farm decays, along straight lines through 0 hours at 100% condition, 20 at 40%, 60 at 15% and 120 (a full rebuild, as a new cottage) at 0%: about 3 hours at 90% and 36 at 30%. Fields gone to grass are cleared like a meadow (10 hours), scrub in 20. Under “the meeting assigns”, the claimant with the most voice-weighted support wins. Decided (the curve), Agreed (rest), 30 Oct.

### Clearing a new plot

The village clears a plot when adults want to move out and there is room. (Decided; details Agreed, next batch)

- **Trigger:** at least one person waiting, nothing claimable, and a free site. Up to two projects per village at a time (Decided, 30 Oct).
- **Room:** a house tile and its first field within about 6 steps of the green, not owned, not the green, not the last commons forest, passable, in the village's own or a neighbouring region. Houses may stand next to each other, and a field need not touch its house, though tiles next to the house are preferred (Decided, 30 Oct). The house stands where the generator would put it, and the field where farmed land may lie: beside a house, off the ring round the green, no nearer the green than its house (Decided, 12 Nov, Kim; Settlements and look, Generated villages).
- **Size:** a new plot is a house and 1 field; the household makes more fields itself (Decided, 30 Oct).
- **Jobs, owned by the village:** clear a meadow tile (10 hours, plus up to 20 for stones) and break the ground (30 hours); fell forest (10 hours, yielding firewood and timber), then grub out the stumps (6 hours); build the cottage in three steps, foundation 30 hours, walls 60 and roof 30, using the timber, so a plot takes most of a year for two or three people (Agreed, 30 Oct).
- **Who works:** role priorities (0–10) of 9 for those waiting for the plot, 7 for farmhands and maids, 5 for sons and daughters and 3 for farmers; landholders treat it as a lower village duty. Labour per person is recorded (Agreed, 30 Oct).
- **Who gets it:** decided by the “cleared land belongs to” custom. Default: the village allots it, married couples first, then by age; a single adult can get one after every couple (Agreed, 30 Oct). A plot cleared or roofed with nobody to have it is allotted afresh at each moving round to one then waiting; after two rounds with nobody, its ground is given back, and what was built on it is left to the weather (Proposed, VB-646, 17 Nov).
- **What you see:** turned soil and stumps, then foundation stones, rising log walls and the turf roof.

The job hours were revised to the long-work scale on 30 October 2026; stones in meadows lengthen clearing.

**Concern:** with one heir per farm and few empty farms, most younger siblings end up as servants or unmarried at home. Clearing new land is the release valve.

### Households short of people (built 1 November; recorded 2 November)

The stability review (`docs/review-stability.md`) added rules that keep a household from dying out for want of hands. Built, numbers in `MARRIAGE`, `SERVICE` and `SETTLE` (`src/sim/rules.js`). (Decided by Kim, 1 Nov; the numbers Agreed, 2 Nov)

- **Marriage of two holders.** Two people who each hold a place may marry; the farm they leave is handed on to an heir who can keep it, else to the landless adult of its village who has waited longest, else it stands empty until claimed (VB-033).
- **The means to marry.** A couple marries only with a year and a half of food in store (`MARRIAGE.means` 1.5); a home with fewer than two adults is exempt.
- **A guardian for an orphaned heir.** A household with no adult takes in a landless relative as guardian, else its children go to kin, who hold the farm in trust until the heir is 18 (below; Decided, Kim 13 Nov, VB-283); only with no kin household within reach does the farm stand empty.
- **A servant for a lone holder.** A holder who lives alone takes in an unmarried landless adult of 18 to 50 from a household that keeps 2 working hands, in its village or within 15 steps. Proposed, VB-281: within a day's walk (12 hours) in place of 15 steps, and the servant walks there.
- **Short of people, checked at the change** (Proposed, VB-283, 10 Nov 2026). Whether a household still has a grown member is checked when its membership changes (a death, a member who leaves, a household founded), not by a daily pass over every household: the change notes the household, and the guardian rule reads only the households noted. Same behaviour, cost by change (backlog VB-323).
- **Taken in before it starves** (Agreed, VB-283, 10 Nov 2026; accepted by Kim, 16 Nov). A household is short of people when at most one of its members is 18 or more and it holds fields: a lone adult, or a minor holder with one grown housemate. The sign is help falling short: when such a household's seasonal ask for help (Goods and the farming year, Help in hunger) leaves it short of food to the next harvest, it is taken in at once. Kin first: its nearest landless grown relative free to move (chosen as a guardian is, by kinship, then the oldest; unmarried, 18 to 50, from a household that keeps a grown member) moves in, with spouse and minor children. Else a servant, by the rule for the lone holder above, at once rather than at the next moving round. People come to the farm, so its owner, holder and fields are unchanged (backlog VB-308). Measured (VB-283, the ten full runs at `38b920d`): 56 of 225 household-years hungry over 0.3 after year 3 are of households short of people (34 households; 35 of them hold one field); a free relative was within reach for 14, a servant for 1.
- **Held by kin when nobody can come** (Decided, Kim 13 Nov 2026, as proposed by VB-283; built by VB-309, `src/sim/trust.js`). When no relative and no servant can come, the household's people move to its nearest kin household within walking reach (parents and children before siblings, as help asks), and that household holds the farm in trust: it works the fields and takes their harvest, while the owner and the holder stay the same; the house stands empty and decays as an empty farm's does. The trust ends when the holder can return (marries, or takes a servant at a moving round), when a ward comes of age, or at the holder's death (succession as today). An orphaned heir whom no guardian can come to is held for the same way, by the kin who take the children, in place of the farm standing empty (the guardian rule above, changed so). 42 of the 56 household-years had nobody to come; 33 of them had a kin household within reach (backlog VB-309).
- **Room.** A new village lies 5 steps or more from other farms and 12 from any village centre; a new farm needs 6 free farmable tiles within reach besides its first field.
- **Names on the map.** A village of two or more farms is labelled once; a lone farm's village bears its farm's name (VB-026).
- **Care is offered and answered** (Proposed, VB-682's plan, 16 Nov; VB-695). The guardian, the household taken in and the farm held by kin stay as above, and become offers that kin make and answer at once (The round as offers): each relative in the order above is asked in turn; a kin head says no only when his own household is hungry (a hunger of 0.5 or more) or when he is at enmity with kin beyond the second degree (**moves** the guardian's rule, Decided 1 and 13 Nov, from a rule to an offer that can meet a no); a refusal of kin in need is remembered (-0.4, 5 years; Kim, 16 Nov), and the people of a dissolved household each ask for themselves. A guardian acts as head of the ward's farm until the heir is 18.

### Moving to new land

When a village is full, its landless adults may leave to found a farm of their own on new land, or join a small village nearby: the daughter settlements (*torp*, *-ryd*, *-boda*) of the medieval colonisation of the outland. Review and sources: `docs/review-expansion.md`. (Decided, 1 Nov: who leaves, fresh land, small villages, distance; Agreed, 2 Nov: deciding, provisions, where, the bonus of fresh land; the rest Proposed) Since 16 November settling is planned in steps (Settling in steps and Sleeping away from home, below; the plan of VB-445, `docs/plans/VB-445.md`): the rows it changes say so, and the code holds the earlier rule until that plan's tasks land.

| Aspect | Design | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Crowded | At the moving round, after plots are started, a village is crowded when adults of it wait for land (no plot, no cottage coming) and no plot site can be found; the village panel shows how many wait | Proposed |
| Who leaves | Landless adults only, with their spouse, their minor children and siblings who follow; holders never leave their farms | Decided, 1 Nov |
| Deciding | A waiting adult of 18–45 in a crowded village leaves at a moving round with a chance of 0.3 after one round of waiting there, 0.6 after two, 0.8 from three; older waiters first. Each unmarried sibling also waiting there follows with a chance of 0.5, at most 2, as farmhand or maid, still landless | Agreed, 2 Nov |
| Provisions | Until the move the party's workers eat rations carried from the households they still belong to, and the rest of the party eats at home as before (Sleeping away from home, below). At the move: food until the next harvest and a week's margin, counted from the day of the move (`SETTLE.margin`, 6 days past the harvest: it was 42 days of the party's rations from the round, 36 of them to the harvest, and 36 left none; a party that moves in mid spring needs about 22), and seed for one field unless the first field is sown already, from the party's stakes and then from their old households' grain above their own seed and food until the harvest (or the holder's buffer, if longer); short of it, the move waits until the grain is there, the cottage standing empty, and the log says why. The party carries it, or carts it where the way takes a cart (backlog VB-675). A household that took in a spouse at this round gives none in it: the grain the marriage was allowed on is not given away the same afternoon (backlog VB-330) | Agreed, 2 Nov (the rule; 42 days from the round); Agreed, 10 Nov (none from a home that just married); Proposed, 16 Nov (counted from the move; VB-445) |
| Where | First a small village: one of 1 to 3 farms, within 6 walking hours of home by the villages' table of hours, so there and back in a day (`SETTLE.join`; it was 30 walking steps), not crowded (room for a plot, none of its own adults waiting, fewer than 2 plots or sites under way), with kin first, then the nearest. Otherwise a new village: a site within 6 hours' walk of the home green, counted along the trail the party would blaze to it (`SETTLE.hours`; it was 10 to 30 walking steps, which were 3.3 to 10 hours when it was decided, at 3 tiles an hour, and 0.8 to 2.5 at 12), and at least 12 steps from every village's centre (`SETTLE.centreApart`, kept as a distance: it is room for two villages' fields, wood and pasture, and at 8 tiles an hour it is 1.5 hours over meadow; the lower bound of 10 walking steps never bound beside it). **When a farm away from home is a new village:** always, at that distance. Three hours there and back is a quarter of the working day, too far to share a green, a well and a work day; nearer ground is the village's own, for its plots (Clearing a new plot). The party's workers walk out and back while they build, and the party walks there at the move, a leg a day (Between villages, A journey; Proposed, VB-281) | Decided (small villages, distance), 1 Nov; Agreed (rest), 2 Nov; Proposed, 16 Nov (both distances in hours, which moves the Decided distance: VB-445, and its question in `QUESTIONS.md`) |
| The new site | Meadow or forest nobody owns (Kim, 15 Nov: they may build a farm of forest; it was meadow only), at least 3 steps from any house, green or field, with a block of 3 free fields and a wood within reach. Wooded ground is scored as the meadow it becomes when grubbed, and a field broken on it is fresh land like any (Fresh land, below). A wooded tile takes 16 hours to fell and grub where a meadow tile takes 10 to 30 to clear of scrub and stones, and the site's own trees are its cottage's timber, so forest is no dearer to settle than meadow by the hour: what it costs is the walk, and the hay meadow and pasture the farm has still to make. The best site by land, wood, water and nearness, the nearness in hours along the trail (`SETTLE.perHour`, 1.2 an hour: it was 0.15 a step), its fields at least 0.9 times as fertile as the home village's. On the forested island free meadow is scarce and the search needs both the forest and the trail: populous at the journey scale, year 0, seeds 7 and 42, with meadow only 0 of 30 villages on medium at seed 7 and 6 of 13 on small find a site (21 of 22 and 12 of 12 at seed 42; every village does on today's island); within 3.75 hours by pathless ground (the 30 steps, over meadow) 1 to 19 villages an island have no site on meadow or forest; within 6 hours along a trail every village has wooded sites, 162 to 317 tiles at the median village (`docs/plans/VB-445.md`, section 2) | Proposed; forest sites on Kim's word of 15 Nov; the numbers Measured, 16 Nov (VB-445) |
| The booth | The party's first work at the site is a booth (*bod*): a shelter of poles, bark and turf, 12 hours and 2 loads of wood, which sleeps 4 (`BOOTH`). A pawn raises it for its household by a verb any pawn has (`raiseBooth`), on ground nobody else holds or has staked, the wood felled on the tile where it is wooded, else fetched from the village's wood; unkept, it is gone in three years (Proposed, VB-784, 17 Nov). Its workers live in it while they clear and build, and their households stay at home until the cottage has its roof: foundation 30 hours, walls 60, with 18 loads of timber, and roof 30 (Settling in steps, below). The booth then stands by the farm and rots as an unkept building does. (It was: the party moves at once into a booth on the site and builds the cottage as its own household's work.) | Proposed, 16 Nov (VB-445) |
| The new village | Founded on the day of the move, when people live there, not at the round: a village of one farm, no green or well, named for its founder (Erikstorp, Olofsboda); it keeps its mother village's customs and culture and records where it came from; its tie to the centre is computed as for any village, so a far daughter drifts more easily and may split off. Its way home is a trail, which fades unless it is walked (Worn paths): a daughter nobody visits is soon twice as far | Proposed |
| Fresh land | A field broken on ground never farmed starts with soil 1.4, above manure's cap of 1.15, and wears 0.08 a crop as any field: on a manured farm it is down to the cap in about 3 crops; without manure it stays 0.4 ahead of an old field until both reach the floor (about 9 crops); in every scenario | Decided, 1 Nov (the bonus); Agreed, 2 Nov (soil 1.4); Proposed (wear) |

#### Settling in steps (Proposed, 16 November; the plan of VB-445)

Kim, 15 November: establishing a farmstead takes several steps, and a way of sleeping away from home. A *torp* was cleared from the mother farm, which fed the clearers; the family followed when there was a roof and a crop in the ground. So the party is formed and the site chosen at the round, the household and the village are founded at the move, and between the two the party's adults are members of their old households who work away from home. Every step is an act of the party's leader or of the pawn who does the work (Principles, Every act is a pawn's), through verbs any pawn has. Hours are of work, at the long-work scale of Clearing a new plot; two workers give about 20 hours a day at the site.

| Step | What is done | Hours | Food | Who |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. The site | At the moving round (autumn day 11) the leader is found as before (Deciding), searches (Where, The new site) and stakes the house tile and the first field. The stake is the leader's, a right, and holds while the work goes on, as a field's stake does (Claims, ownership and territory); nobody moves | None | None: all eat at home | The leader; the party is the leader, the spouse and the siblings who follow, as before |
| 2. The trail | From the day after the round, a trail is blazed along the route to the site wherever it crosses wood with no path: each such tile is cut and marked and becomes a path (Worn paths: its count is set to a path's, and it halves a year as any wear does). Nothing is blazed on open ground, which wears its own way, and no trail carries a cart. A site with no wood between needs none | 3 a wooded tile, 1 where the tile is still trodden (`PATHS.blaze`); at most about 35 tiles and 105 hours, the route being at most 6 hours long; a site 4.5 hours out with 15 wooded tiles on its way, 45 hours | Meals at home while the trail's head is within the daily walk, then carried rations (Sleeping away from home) | The party's adults |
| 3. The booth and the clearing | The booth first (The booth). Then the house tile and the first field are cleared: felled and grubbed where they are wooded, cleared of scrub and stones where they are meadow; the field is broken, and sown when its season comes, with seed carried out. The cottage: foundation, walls, roof; its timber is the site's felled trees, or is felled in the nearest wood where the site is meadow. Each job runs in its own season, as on any farm; with nothing open the workers go home and work there | Booth 12. House tile: 16 wooded, 10 to 30 meadow. First field: 46 wooded (fell 10, grub 6, break 30), 40 to 60 meadow. Cottage 120. In all about 195 hours on forest and 180 to 220 on meadow, with timber trips besides: ten days for two workers, a season with the trail and the walks | Carried rations, a pack of at most 12 days (`AWAY.pack`) from the household each still belongs to; a worker walks home for the next | The party's members of 14 and over, but a mother who nurses; the site's jobs rank 9 for them, as a plot's do for those who wait for it. Nobody else: help from kin at a clearing is a custom to come |
| 4. The move | When the roof is on and the first field is broken (sown, or ready for its season), and the provisions are there (Provisions): the household is founded, with the leader as its head, and the village, where the site is a new one. The party walks out with its loads, children and all, as a journey of one leg; what it cannot carry is its own at the old farm until fetched (VB-675) | None but the walks | Provisions to the harvest and a week, and seed | The whole party |
| If it fails | A leader who dies is followed by the party's next adult. A party that ends (its leader wed onto a holding, given a plot, or dead with no adult left) leaves its stake, and the stake lapses as any does: the site keeps the work done on it, and the next party that searches finds a site half made. A cottage with its roof on and no household is an empty farm (Empty farms) | | | |
| The played pawn | Has what any leader or party member has: the wish to lead or follow (VB-392, on every pawn's wishes, VB-680, and the party's call, VB-696); as leader, which of the search's best sites to stake (`SETTLE.shown`, 5: a leader not played takes the first, the one its judgement ranks best); the site's jobs, taken as any job; when to walk out and when to come home; and the day of the move, from the first day it may be made (a leader not played moves on that day). As head of a household that feeds a worker it has no new act: the rations are a member's | | | |

Cost, named before building (Rules for cost, 9). The search: one bounded search from the green for each crowded village where someone leads, at most 7,057 tiles (6 hours over open meadow) where a village's row of the table of hours searches to 67 hours; a round, not a day. Sites under way: one a party, from the round to the move; populous small founded or joined about one farm a year (`docs/review-expansion.md`, section 6), so a few sites and under ten pawns away on an island of 1,000. A site has at most six jobs open; a trail at most 35 tiles. Nothing is polled: the move is tried when the roof's job ends and on its leader's household's planning day.

#### Sleeping away from home (Proposed, 16 November; the plan of VB-445)

One mechanism for every night a pawn spends away from its household's house: a journey's nights on the road (Between villages, A journey), the settlers' booth, the suitor's visit, a carrier who arrives late, and later a far wood or a summer pasture worked from a booth. It takes over the journey's state when it is built; a journey is then a stay away whose bed is where the walker stops.

| Aspect | Design |
| --- | --- |
| The bed | A pawn has a bed: the tile its day starts from and ends at. It is its household's house unless the pawn is away; then it is where it sleeps. Whatever reads "home" for a walk or a job's reach reads the bed |
| Who may sleep where | **On the road:** anyone on a journey, where the day's leg ends. **In a booth:** those of the party or household that built it, and whom they let in (as built, the guests its maker names when it is staked, and any its maker lets in later by the verb `letIn`, a right that holds at once: Proposed, VB-784, 17 Nov; VB-984, 19 Nov), 4 to a booth. **As a guest:** in another household's house, with its head's yes (the suitor's visit; a carrier; kin; a head who does not dislike the guest, its opinion of it not below 0, says yes: Proposed, VB-783, 17 Nov) |
| Food | A pawn away eats what it carries, as a journey's provisions: taken when it sets out from its household's food above its seed, a ration for each day planned, at most 12 (`AWAY.pack`, of the 30 rations a back carries), eaten uncooked, so a ration away costs a third more grain than one at the table (1 / `ECON.uncooked`). It is left out of its household's meals, by one test where the journey's was. What is left of the pack comes home with it, as the grain it was taken as, crop by crop, back into the household's grain (not its meals); a pawn who dies away leaves it lying where it fell, for its household to fetch (Proposed, VB-962, 17 Nov). A household that cannot spare the whole pack sends nobody (Proposed, VB-783, 17 Nov; it was: with nothing to spare): the worker stays and works at home until there is |
| The working day | 6:00 to 18:00, as at home, and all of it for the work the pawn is away for: no walk out and back, no chores of the house, no occasion at home. Whether a place is worked from home or from a bed there is judged by the walk: lodge when the walk there and back is more than a third of the working day (`AWAY.walk`, 4 hours), by the pawn's estimate of the route; with VB-781 this becomes part of the head's judgement of the walk |
| Coming home | When the work is done or none is open; when the pack is down to the walk home's ration; when it is ill or has gone hungry; when its household changes (it wed, its head died). Checked at the day's meal and when its job ends, never by a pass over pawns. The walk home's ration is a ration a night still on the road, none when it is home by the day's end; ill is health below `HEALTH.riskBelow` (50); gone hungry is a meal from the pack that fell short; a changed household turns it home at once (Proposed, VB-783, 17 Nov) |
| A night without a roof | A bed with no roof and no fire is cold and wet by the rules of an unheated house (Goods and the farming year, Fuel): a night on the road in winter costs health as a night at home without wood does, and in summer less, not nothing (two summer nights on the road cost about half of two winter ones), since a house with no wood is cold and wet in every season (Proposed, VB-785, 17 Nov). A booth is dry, and warm with wood at hand (a load burns a night for all in it); a guest is as warm as its host. So a long winter trip is dear, and a stay of more than a night or two wants a booth |
| State and owner | `st.away[]`, the open stays, owned by `sim/away` (a new module that takes the journeys out of `sim/agents`, as VB-422 meant to split them): each `{id, pawn, why, bed (a tile, or none on the road), roof ('none', 'booth' or 'house'), food, from, to}`, plain data, with the journeys' ids and due legs; `agent.away` indexes a pawn's stay. `st.booths[]`, owned by `sim/building`: `{id, tile, by, built, cond, condAt}`, rotting lazily as a house's condition does. A save's `st.journeys` loads as stays. As built (Proposed, VB-783, VB-784, VB-962, 17 Nov): a stay's pack holds the grain by crop; `sim/agents` keeps the walking and the bed, and tells `sim/away` of a leg's end, a death and an arrival by events; a booth is `{id, tile, by (the household), maker (the pawn who staked it), guests, built, wood, cond, condAt}`, and `st.booths` holds the live ones only |
| Cost | Entities: the pawns away, tens on an island of 1,000 (about 26 journeys a year at `medium-ref`, a few sites' workers). Cadence: a meal a day each, from the pack; a leg when one is due. Work: constant a call. No loop over pawns is added: the household's meal skips a member by its index |

### Households and other bodies (slice 5, 11 November)

Proposed by slice 5's plan (backlog VB-184, 11 November 2026), from `docs/slices.md` 3.5 and target-v2 (4.4, 4.6, trace 7.1). A body is
what holds land, owes and is owed: in slice 5 the household and the village; the alderman is the village's first office. Its
answers stand as Agreed (Kim, 12 Nov; `QUESTION_ARCHIVE.md`, VB-184); only the membership graph is open (`QUESTIONS.md`, VB-184, question 7).

| Aspect | Design | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Bodies | Households, villages, parishes, courts, estates, workshops, crews and ships as bodies, with members along typed role edges; a body exists only when something needs its own identity. One membership graph with rules per kind is Proposed: it passed the succession probe (target-v2, section 9). Measured on 11 November (VB-184; populous medium, seed 7, year 12: 1,031 living, 1,288 ever born, 211 live households): the graph of today's memberships (residence, service, holding, a household's village, unions; the dead's ended edges kept outside the live index) has 2,009 edges, builds in 0.55 ms, and the household cache derived from it in 0.33 ms, equal to today's for every household; a move (an edge ended, one made, the cache kept) costs under 1 µs; VB-043's bound is 50 ms. Proposed: adopt the graph, with the household cache as Membership's own (`QUESTIONS.md`, VB-184, question 7) | Open (Decided, 3 Nov: iterate before deciding); measured, 11 Nov |
